## Declined (186)

- `gad` (person, 45 verses): the second reading dropped every clause: The verses tell of the children of Gad, the tribe, not Gad the son of Jacob himself.; The altar was built and named by the descendants, the children of Gad, not by Gad the son of Jacob.
- `merari` (person, 38 verses): The verses name Merari only in genealogies and lists, and the tabernacle duties and cities they describe belong to his descendants, the sons of Merari, rather than to him.
- `dan` (person, 32 verses): The verses about Dan himself only record his birth and name, which the summary already gives as son of Bilhah, and the rest are lists, censuses and deeds of his tribe.
- `nun` (person, 30 verses): Every verse names Nun only as the father of Joshua (or in a genealogy), and the deeds described are Joshua's, so nothing is said of Nun himself beyond the summary.
- `kohath` (person, 29 verses): The verses about Kohath only list him among Levi's sons, give his sons, his age at death and the allotments to his descendants, and tell no deed of his own or event that happened to him beyond what the summary already says.
- `asher` (person, 26 verses): The verses give only his birth and naming, genealogies, tribal censuses and lists, and a blessing pronounced on him, none of which is a deed or event beyond what the summary already says.
- `nebat` (person, 26 verses): Nebat is named only as the father of Jeroboam in verses that tell of Jeroboam's deeds, and nothing is stated of Nebat himself beyond what the summary says.
- `zeruiah` (person, 25 verses): Every verse names Zeruiah only to identify her sons Joab, Abishai and Asahel or her siblings, and none states anything she herself did or that happened to her.
- `gershon` (person, 24 verses): The verses only list Gershon among Levi's sons and speak of his descendants, their numbers, duties and cities, and say nothing of Gershon himself beyond what the summary already gives.
- `zebulun` (person, 22 verses): The verses mostly list Zebulun in genealogies, censuses, camp orders and town lists, or pronounce a blessing, and his birth and naming say no more than the summary already does.
- `issachar` (person, 21 verses): The verses only record his birth and naming, which the summary already covers as son of Leah, and otherwise list his sons, the tribe's numbers, lots and cities; the tribe's deeds are not his own.
- `ner` (person, 16 verses): Ner appears only in genealogies and as the father of Abner, so the verses say no more of him than the summary's family relations do.
- `nethaniah` (person, 16 verses): The verses name Nethaniah only as the father of Ishmael, who is the one who acts, so they say no more of him than the summary already does.
- `jephunneh` (person, 15 verses): Every marked verse names Jephunneh only as the father of Caleb, so the deeds belong to Caleb and nothing is told of Jephunneh himself beyond the summary.
- `kareah` (person, 14 verses): Kareah is named in these verses only as the father of Johanan and Jonathan, and every deed belongs to his son Johanan, so nothing is said of Kareah himself beyond the summary.
- `amoz` (person, 13 verses): Amoz is named in these verses only as the father of Isaiah, and every deed in them belongs to Isaiah, so nothing is said of Amoz beyond what the summary already gives.
- `hezron-2` (person, 13 verses): The verses mostly list Hezron in genealogies; the marriage to Machir's daughter is already in the summary, and his death in Calebephratah is too slight to name him for.
- `remaliah` (person, 13 verses): Every verse names Remaliah only as the father of Pekah, so nothing is said of Remaliah himself beyond the summary.
- `caesar` (person, 12 verses): The verses only mention Caesar as the one owed tribute, as the name on the coin and as a title invoked in accusations, and they record nothing he did or that happened to him.
- `heth` (person, 12 verses): The verses only list Heth in genealogies and refer to his descendants, the sons of Heth, in Abraham's purchase of a burying place, so nothing is said of Heth himself beyond the summary.
- `nahor-2` (person, 12 verses): The verses only list Nahor in genealogies and relations (son, brother, husband, father, grandfather) and name him in passing, saying nothing of his own deeds beyond the summary.
- `shallum-7` (person, 12 verses): The verses only list Shallum among the gatekeepers and their descendants, and several marked verses concern other men of the name, so they say no more than the summary already does.
- `amminadab` (person, 11 verses): Every verse naming Amminadab only places him in a genealogy or identifies him as the father of Nahshon or Elisheba, which the summary already states, and none records anything he did or that happened to him.
- `amram` (person, 11 verses): The verses only list Amram in Levite genealogies, giving his parentage, his wife, his children and his age, all of which the summary already records, and they report no deed or event of his own.
- `eber` (person, 11 verses): The verses only place Eber in genealogies and give his lifespan and children, which the summary already covers, and record no deed or event of his own.
- `gilead` (person, 11 verses): The verses mention Gilead only in genealogies and tribal lists, giving his sons and descendants and nothing he did or that happened to him.
- `shealtiel-2` (person, 11 verses): The verses name Shealtiel only as Zerubbabel's father or as a link in genealogies, and every deed in them belongs to Zerubbabel, so they say no more than the summary does.
- `uzziel` (person, 11 verses): The verses marked for Uzziel only list him and his descendants in genealogies and Levite rolls, and say nothing he did or that happened to him beyond what the summary already gives.
- `arpachshad` (person, 10 verses): The verses only list Arphaxad in genealogies and give his years and offspring, which the summary already covers, with no deed or event of his own.
- `caesar-2` (person, 10 verses): The verses only mention Caesar as the one Paul appealed to and was to be sent to, which the summary already says, and record no deed or event of his own.
- `izhar` (person, 10 verses): The verses only list Izhar among the sons of Kohath and give his descendants in genealogies, and say nothing of anything he did or that happened to him.
- `neriah` (person, 10 verses): The verses name Neriah only as the father of Baruch and Seraiah, and everything they tell is about his sons, which the summary already covers.
- `shelah` (person, 10 verses): The verses only place Shelah in genealogies, giving his age at fatherhood and years lived, which add nothing beyond the summary's relations.
- `anak` (person, 9 verses): The verses name Anak only as the ancestor or father of the Anakim and his three sons, and the events they tell (the spies' fear, the sons' expulsion from Hebron) belong to his descendants, not to him.
- `beor-2` (person, 9 verses): Every marked verse names Beor only as the father of Balaam, so they say nothing of Beor himself beyond the summary; the deeds belong to Balaam.
- `bichri` (person, 8 verses): Bichri appears in these verses only as the father named in 'Sheba the son of Bichri', so the verses say nothing of him beyond the summary's 'father of Sheba'.
- `chelubai` (person, 8 verses): The verses only list Chelubai (Caleb) in genealogies of sons, wives and descendants, and state no deed or event of his own.
- `eliphaz` (person, 8 verses): The verses only list Eliphaz's birth, sons and descendants in genealogies, which the summary already records, and state no deed or event of his own.
- `enosh` (person, 8 verses): The verses give only Enosh's birth, his fathering of Kenan, his age at death and his place in genealogies, which the summary already covers; the remark that men began to call on the LORD is a statement about men in his time, not a deed of his.
- `kenan` (person, 8 verses): The verses only place Kenan in a genealogy, giving his sons' births and age at death, which adds nothing beyond the summary.
- `lamech-2` (person, 8 verses): The verses only give Lamech's genealogy, his age at Noah's birth and his death, which add nothing beyond the summary's relations.
- `libni` (person, 8 verses): The verses only list Libni among the sons of Gershon and in a line of descent, saying nothing of any deed or event of his, and the marked verses 1CH 23:7-9 and 26:21 concern Laadan and Shimei rather than him.
- `mahli` (person, 8 verses): The verses only list Mahli in genealogies of the Levites and name his sons, saying nothing he did or that happened to him beyond what the summary already gives.
- `mushi` (person, 8 verses): The verses only list Mushi in genealogies of the Levites, giving his father and sons, which the summary already states, and tell no deed or event of him.
- `peleg` (person, 8 verses): The verses only record Peleg in genealogies and name him for the earth being divided in his days, which is a note on the name and not a deed or event of his, and the rest repeats the summary's relations.
- `rechab-2` (person, 8 verses): The verses name Rechab only as the father of Jehonadab or the ancestor of the Rechabites and record no deed or event of Rechab himself, so they say no more than the summary does.
- `bela-2` (person, 7 verses): The verses only list Bela among the sons of Benjamin and name his sons and families, which the summary already gives, and tell no deed or event of him.
- `elam-4` (person, 7 verses): The verses only list Elam as a family head in census counts, lists of returning households and lists of signers or participants, and say nothing of anything the man himself did.
- `hammedatha` (person, 7 verses): Hammedatha appears only as the father of Haman in the verses given, so nothing is said of him beyond what the summary already states.
- `hebron` (person, 7 verses): The verses only list Hebron among the sons of Kohath and name his descendants in genealogies and Levite rosters, which says no more than the summary does.
- `hilkiah` (person, 7 verses): Hilkiah appears in these verses only as the father of Eliakim, which the summary already states, and nothing is told of what he himself did.
- `immer` (person, 7 verses): The verses only name Immer in genealogies, a course list, census totals and as father of Pashur; they record no deed or event of Immer himself.
- `jared` (person, 7 verses): The verses only place Jared in a genealogy, giving his father, his son, his years and his death, which the summary already covers or which is merely a list entry.
- `mahalalel` (person, 7 verses): The verses only place Mahalaleel in a genealogy, giving his sons, his age and his death, which the summary already covers or which is no deed or event beyond the list.
- `methuselah` (person, 7 verses): The verses only place Methuselah in a genealogy, giving his sons, his years and his death, which adds nothing beyond the summary.
- `raphah` (person, 7 verses): The verses mention him only as the giant to whom four Philistine warriors were born, which is what the summary already says, and the deeds recorded are those of his sons and their slayers.
- `zibeon-2` (person, 7 verses): The verses only list Zibeon in genealogies and lists of Horite sons and dukes, saying nothing of his own deeds beyond what the summary already gives.
- `zippor` (person, 7 verses): Zippor appears only as the father in the name "Balak the son of Zippor", and no verse tells anything he did or that happened to him.
- `adah-wife-of-esau` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Adah as Esau's wife and the mother of Eliphaz in a genealogy, which the summary already gives, so no further deed or event is stated.
- `ammihud` (person, 6 verses): The verses only name Ammihud as the father of Elishama and in a genealogy, and say nothing he did or that happened to him.
- `ashbel` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Ashbel among the sons of Benjamin and as head of a family in genealogies and a census, saying nothing more than the summary does.
- `bani-4` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Bani's descendants in a register, a list of men with foreign wives and a list of chiefs, and tell nothing of any deed or event of his own.
- `bigvai` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Bigvai among returning leaders, family head counts, and signatories, and the only deed (coming with Zerubbabel) is already in the summary.
- `cush` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Cush among the sons of Ham and name his own sons, which the summary already states, and tell no deed of his own.
- `deuel` (person, 6 verses): The verses mention Deuel only as the father of Eliasaph in lists of tribal princes and offerings, which says no more than the summary already does.
- `elkanah-4` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Elkanah in genealogies of Levite families and state no deed or event beyond the fatherhood and sonship already in the summary.
- `gera-3` (person, 6 verses): The verses only name Gera as the father of Ehud or Shimei or as a son of Bela in a genealogy, and tell nothing that he himself did or that happened to him.
- `harim-3` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Harim in priestly courses, a census of returning families, a list of those with foreign wives and a list of sealers, none of which says more than the summary does.
- `iddo-5` (person, 6 verses): The marked verses only name Iddo as an ancestor of Zechariah or list him among priestly heads, so they say no more of him than the summary already does.
- `jedaiah-2` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Jedaiah among priests, returning families and heads of priestly houses, and tell no deed or event beyond what the summary already says.
- `jerahmeel` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Jerahmeel's sons, wives and descendants in a genealogy and record no deed or event of his own beyond what the summary already says.
- `joktan` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Joktan in a genealogy as Eber's son, Peleg's brother and father of thirteen sons, which the summary already states.
- `mahalath` (person, 6 verses): The verses only record her marriage to Esau and her family ties, which the summary already gives, and the marked verses in Genesis 36 concern Bashemath, whose name differs.
- `nahor` (person, 6 verses): The verses only give Nahor's place in a genealogy, with his age at fatherhood and years lived, which adds nothing to the summary beyond relations.
- `obed` (person, 6 verses): The verses only name Obed in genealogies and at his naming, saying no more than the summary's relations to Boaz, Ruth, Jesse and David.
- `pashhur-the-priest` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Pashur in genealogies, returnee counts, a list of those who sealed, and a list of those who married foreign wives, so they add no deed or event beyond the summary.
- `ram` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Ram in genealogies, saying no more of him than the summary already does.
- `reu` (person, 6 verses): The verses only place Reu in genealogies, giving his lifespan and his sons, which the summary already covers, and record no deed or event of his.
- `reuel` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Reuel in genealogies of Esau's sons and descendants, saying no more than the summary already does.
- `salmon` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Salmon in genealogies as son of Nahshon and father of Boaz, which the summary already states, and record no deed or event.
- `serug` (person, 6 verses): The verses only give Serug's place in a genealogy (his age at the birth of Nahor and that he had other children), which adds nothing beyond the summary.
- `shemaiah-19` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Shemaiah among priests and among those in the dedication procession, and several of the marked verses may concern other men of the name, so no deed of this priest is stated.
- `shimei` (person, 6 verses): The verses only list Shimei among the sons of Gershon and name his sons, which the summary already states, and tell no deed or event of him.
